I mean, my clothes seem to fit about the same. I mean, I’ve had moments where I’m tired of feeling keyed up or guilty, but not thoughts of hurting myself. I wouldn’t want you to think it’s gone there.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself worrying about things that might happen in the future?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I suppose I do, though I try not to make too much of it. The sailing races, the Saturday historical society shift, working in the woodshop—those used to kind of keep me steady. Lately they just feel like chores I keep putting off. I don’t mean to sound dramatic, but that’s really what made me agree to come in.

I don’t want to make it sound like I get nothing out of them at all. But mostly… sailing, the historical society shift, even working in the woodshop, it just feels like I’m pushing myself through it. Like I know those things used to keep me balanced, and now they feel more like chores I’m supposed to do. I keep putting them off, which isn’t really like me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, my clothes seem to fit about the same. I haven’t been tracking it closely or anything, but I don’t think there’s been any significant weight change.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve had moments where I’m tired of feeling keyed up or guilty, but not thoughts of hurting myself. I wouldn’t want you to think it’s gone there.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself worrying about things that might happen in the future?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I suppose I do, though I try not to make too much of it. It’s not like I’m sitting there inventing disasters all day, but there’s this kind of low-grade dread that follows me around. And guilt, too, like I should’ve seen one more risk coming, even when people keep telling me the controls were in place. I don’t know if that answers it the right way, but… yes, I do worry about what could happen.\n\nDoctor: Do you still experience pleasure when you're doing things that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I still can enjoy things a little once I’m there, maybe. But honestly, that’s part of why I finally agreed to come in. The things that used to kind of keep me steady—sailing races, my Saturday shift at the historical society, tinkering around in the woodshop—they all feel more like chores now. I keep putting them off.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but… I just don’t seem to care about them the way I used to.\n\nDoctor: Have you had periods like this before where you lost interest in things and felt down, or is this the first time you've experienced something like this?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’ve really had it quite like this before. I mean, I’ve had busy stretches where I skipped things or felt worn out, sure, but this feels different.\n\nWhat finally made me agree to come in is that I just don’t care much about the things that used to keep me balanced—sailing races, my Saturday shift at the historical society, even tinkering in the woodshop. They all feel like chores I keep putting off. I hope that makes sense… I don’t want to overstate it, but it’s not really like me.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still look forward to or enjoy as much as you used to?\n\nPatient: A few things are… okay once I get myself to do them, I guess. But I wouldn’t say I look forward to much the way I used to.\n\nThe sailing races, the Saturday historical society shift, working in the woodshop—those used to kind of keep me steady. Lately they just feel like chores I keep putting off. I don’t mean to sound dramatic, but that’s really what made me agree to come in.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or enjoy without feeling forced to do them?\n\nPatient: Honestly… not many without having to push myself a bit. I can sometimes get some pleasure once I’m doing something, but it doesn’t come naturally like it used to.\n\nThat’s really what made me agree to come in, I guess. The sailing races, my Saturday shift at the historical society, even tinkering in the woodshop—those used to keep me balanced, and now they feel more like chores I keep putting off. I don’t know if that’s the kind of answer you mean.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that bring you joy or pleasure without you having to force yourself to do them?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I wish I had a better answer. I kept telling myself it was just stress and things would settle, but it hasn’t really gone back to normal.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the time since you lost interest in those activities?\n\nPatient: Not sad exactly, or… I wouldn’t want to overstate it. I mean… I’m trying to think of one, but honestly, not really.

It’s not that I can’t do things at all. I just don’t really care about the things that used to keep me level—sailing races, my Saturday shift at the historical society, even tinkering in the woodshop. They all feel more like chores I keep putting off than things I’m glad to do.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I guess… what finally got me to agree to come in is that I’ve just sort of lost interest in the things that usually keep me steady. It’s not like everything has fallen apart, exactly, but sailing races, my Saturday shift at the historical society, even messing around in the woodshop… they all feel more like chores now, and I keep putting them off. I’m not sure if that’s the kind of thing you’re asking about.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way about the things that usually interest you?\n\nPatient: I think it’s been… a little over a year now. It seemed to start after the scaffold accident, during that whole business readjustment period. I kept telling myself it was just stress and things would settle, but it hasn’t really gone back to normal.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the time since you lost interest in those activities?\n\nPatient: Not sad exactly, or… I wouldn’t want to overstate it. It’s more like this low-level dread that’s just kind of there, and guilt too. Like I keep thinking I should’ve seen one more risk coming, even though people keep telling me the controls were in place. I don’t know if that counts as “down,” but it does follow me around more than I’d like.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy things when you're actually doing them, even if you don't feel like starting them?\n\nPatient: A little, sometimes, once I’m actually there. I don’t want to make it sound like I get nothing out of them at all. But mostly… sailing, the historical society shift, even working in the woodshop, it just feels like I’m pushing myself through it. Like I know those things used to keep me balanced, and now they feel more like chores I’m supposed to do. I keep putting them off, which isn’t really like me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, my clothes seem to fit about the same. I haven’t been tracking it closely or anything, but I don’t think there’s been any significant weight change.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve had moments where I’m tired of feeling keyed up or guilty, but not thoughts of hurting myself. I wouldn’t want you to think it’s gone there.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself worrying about things that might happen in the future?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I suppose I do, though I try not to make too much of it. It’s not like I’m sitting there inventing disasters all day, but there’s this kind of low-grade dread that follows me around. And guilt, too, like I should’ve seen one more risk coming, even when people keep telling me the controls were in place. I don’t know if that answers it the right way, but… yes, I do worry about what could happen.\n\nDoctor: Do you still experience pleasure when you're doing things that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I still can enjoy things a little once I’m there, maybe. But honestly, that’s part of why I finally agreed to come in. The things that used to kind of keep me steady—sailing races, my Saturday shift at the historical society, tinkering around in the woodshop—they all feel more like chores now. I keep putting them off.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but… I just don’t seem to care about them the way I used to.\n\nDoctor: Have you had periods like this before where you lost interest in things and felt down, or is this the first time you've experienced something like this?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’ve really had it quite like this before. I mean, I’ve had busy stretches where I skipped things or felt worn out, sure, but this feels different.\n\nWhat finally made me agree to come in is that I just don’t care much about the things that used to keep me balanced—sailing races, my Saturday shift at the historical society, even tinkering in the woodshop. They all feel like chores I keep putting off. I hope that makes sense… I don’t want to overstate it, but it’s not really like me.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still look forward to or enjoy as much as you used to?\n\nPatient: A few things are… okay once I get myself to do them, I guess. But I wouldn’t say I look forward to much the way I used to.\n\nThe sailing races, the Saturday historical society shift, working in the woodshop—those used to kind of keep me steady. Lately they just feel like chores I keep putting off. I don’t mean to sound dramatic, but that’s really what made me agree to come in.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or enjoy without feeling forced to do them?\n\nPatient: Honestly… not many without having to push myself a bit. I can sometimes get some pleasure once I’m doing something, but it doesn’t come naturally like it used to.\n\nThat’s really what made me agree to come in, I guess. The sailing races, my Saturday shift at the historical society, even tinkering in the woodshop—those used to keep me balanced, and now they feel more like chores I keep putting off. I don’t know if that’s the kind of answer you mean.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that bring you joy or pleasure without you having to force yourself to do them?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I wish I had a better answer. I don’t know if that answers it the right way, but… yes, I do worry about what could happen.\n\nDoctor: Do you still experience pleasure when you're doing things that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I still can enjoy things a little once I’m there, maybe. But honestly, that’s part of why I finally agreed to come in. The things that used to kind of keep me steady—sailing races, my Saturday shift at the historical society, tinkering around in the woodshop—they all feel more like chores now. I keep putting them off.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but… I just don’t seem to care about them the way I used to.\n\nDoctor: Have you had periods like this before where you lost interest in things and felt down, or is this the first time you've experienced something like this?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’ve really had it quite like this before. I mean, I’ve had busy stretches where I skipped things or felt worn out, sure, but this feels different.\n\nWhat finally made me agree to come in is that I just don’t care much about the things that used to keep me balanced—sailing races, my Saturday shift at the historical society, even tinkering in the woodshop. They all feel like chores I keep putting off. I hope that makes sense… I don’t want to overstate it, but it’s not really like me.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still look forward to or enjoy as much as you used to?\n\nPatient: A few things are… okay once I get myself to do them, I guess. But I wouldn’t say I look forward to much the way I used to.\n\nThe sailing races, the Saturday historical society shift, working in the woodshop—those used to kind of keep me steady. Lately they just feel like chores I keep putting off.
